## Dates

Each commit is dated at noon (America/New_York) on the day the change took effect. The other dates, enacted and published, are trailers.

When a law gives no date of its own, the default rule applies: No dated history is published: each act is the oldest version available, dated by the source's own currency statement (the lastmod date of the site's sitemap). https://code.wvlegislature.gov/sitemap.xml

## How the history is built

The same corpus always gives the same commit hashes. `tools/check_repo.py` reads every version back from git and compares it with the corpus. `tools/on.py` shows the law as it stood on any date. The corpus format is described in `tools/SCHEMA.md`.

- Corpus: sha256 `5db389148bfaa648e7401147a60f03ca76fd8be3ec2e74c9312988b6995b392f` (61979817 bytes).
- Builder blob hashes (`git hash-object`): `build.py` `8703f1dc3e9750c069dea44ed21f1a1bed63179d`, `check.py` `e340718c28cd5b0edbe37bdeab1aac31427ada66`, `on.py` `4a8aa1c9a5e6bd8cc831b90a6e68c5a6f448a13c`.
- Python 3.12.10; git version 2.46.0.windows.1.

## Decisions made by hand

- Each act is a single "oldest version available": code.wvlegislature.gov publishes only the current text, with no dated history.
- All acts are dated 2026-10-13, the lastmod date the site's own sitemap.xml gives every page (the code is "updated regularly as new legislation takes effect").
- One act per article of a chapter; one unit per section, keyed by the section number the site prints.
- Only the section text is taken. The site's bill-history and signed-bill widgets are not part of the statute text and are not copied.
- Repealed sections keep the lines the site prints for them (heading, "Repealed", and the repealing Act).
- The West Virginia Constitution is not included: it is not used in this record.
- Wave 4 (2026-11-10): every chapter other than 61, 31D and 36 is read from the Legislature's own article PDFs (code.wvlegislature.gov/pdf/<chapter>-<article>/), one act per article, one unit per section, dated 2026-10-13, the lastmod the sitemap gives every page. Each text block of the PDF is one paragraph; running heads, page footers and the "WV Legislature" mark are left out. Each article's section numbers were checked against the section URLs the sitemap lists.
- The Constitution of West Virginia (one page on www.wvlegislature.gov, one unit per section, the preamble and the numbered amendments) is dated 2026-11-10, the day it was retrieved: the page states no currency date. It replaces the first build's note that the Constitution is not included.
- 52 sections that the sitemap lists but an article PDF leaves out (mostly repealed sections, and 5A-3-38 to 5A-3-55A) are read from their own section pages, as chapters 61, 31D and 36 are. Section 8-29-8A of the sitemap is printed 8-29B-8a on its page and in the PDF; it is kept once, as printed.
- Where an article PDF prints a section twice (33-6-15a twice; 60-3-1 in its current and its preceding version), both are kept, the second with a -dup2 key.
- Check: a fixed random sample of 80 sections of the PDF chapters was compared with their section pages: 79 have the same text (spacing before punctuation aside); in one (48-31-401) the section page shows only the heading while the PDF has the full text.
- Earlier entries are complete Code articles as enacted in the cited Acts volumes. Dates use the printed commencement statement, including its stated number of days after passage. Current published wording is retained; intervening consolidations are not reconstructed.

## What AI did and did not do

The source readers and builders were developed under Lexifina's direction with coding assistants, including Claude Code and Codex.

No model wrote, translated or corrected legal text. No model runs at build time. Every word of law in this repository comes from the official sources above.

The commit messages are written by `build.py` from the source data.

Model use at data-build time: - none
- A model wrote extraction and verification code; no model-generated legal wording was used.
